Only current Tables A–C, the newest owner decisions and autonomous workflow govern future execution.

## 1. Authority, gameplay and privacy

1. **Server authority:** Real rules, attack legality, Duel continuation, Negation eligibility, damage, deadlines and card conservation remain unchanged. `CurrentAction` alone controls private selectable Hand cards, legal providers, Confirm, Cancel, Skip and End. Never reconstruct legal actions in React.
2. **Public event authority:** `PresentationV2`, `PresentationSnapshot` and validated public events/proofs determine genuine public card identity, played-as conversion, actor, target, committed order and settlement. Do not derive identity or causality from seat index, player name, HP change, latest animation, inferred Hand composition or merely observing a private selection. A private selected card **must not** become a public reveal.
3. **Privacy:** An opponent's Hand content, private response opportunities, hidden roles, anonymous target-card identities and skill options stay secret. Public Hand **count**, public Equipment, public Judgment and public skills may be displayed where permitted. A host may control only server-authorized test players, not take over a human participant.
4. **Seat ownership:** Actual opponent Seats and the Local Player Dock stay fixed. A public card parks in front of its **server-proven actor's** physical Seat/Dock on *each viewer's* screen; source and target are facts, never inferred from geometry.
5. **One public copy:** A committed card may change visible position/scale while transitioning but must not be simultaneously duplicated by a legacy animation, old Stage and new presenter. Any public information missing validation **fails closed**, with a readable neutral waiting state when needed, never a fictitious CardFace or an incorrect source/target.
6. **No gameplay blocking:** Presentation may not delay or stretch authoritative response windows, turn progression, damage, card submission or network updates. Timers continue from the server deadline.
7. **One-screen interaction:** The player must still see their Hand, Skills, response Guidance, timer and legal buttons. Public card animation is *informational only*; card reveal/parking must never steal interaction focus or intercept touches.

## 2. Battlefield layout — iPhone-first

The screen has three stable areas:

| Area | Responsibility | Placement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Physical opponent Seats | Who is at the table; HP, public information, available test-player Switch | Fixed top-row or side-column topology |
| **Central battlefield** | **At most one active, newly committed public CardFace** and one short true public event message | Centred within measured safe usable battlefield, not over Seats or Guidance |
| Local Player Dock | The viewer's Hero, Skills, Equipment, private Hand and `CurrentAction` commands | Fixed at bottom; never displaced by a reveal |

### 2.1 Centre card

- During **REVEAL**, use a **readable, measured local Hand CardFace size** that is clearly larger than a parked thumbnail. Earlier code used 60×90 for Attack and 90×135 for Duel; those are historical code measurements, NOT two mandated sizes. Verify actual dimensions on phones under §8.7. Keep true art, suit, rank and identity, without a full-Stage poster.
- Place it at the measured visual centre of the usable battlefield, not halfway down the full phone viewport. Keep readable whitespace and a short message such as `Cao Cao used Attack on Zhao Yun`, using real public actor/target identifiers. The central slot must clear before the next reveal.
- No duplicated central player/target illustration; player identity remains anchored in the genuine seat.

### 2.2 Seat-front public card thumbnails

- After centre reveal, shrink **the same public card instance** to an initial medium thumbnail of approximately **48×72 CSS px** (or 50×75) with original 2:3 proportion and readable rank/suit/name.
- **Opponents in top row:** place the thumbnail **just below their actual Hero Seat**, clearly associated with that player without covering HP, name, Hand count, VIEW or SWITCH.
- **Side-column opponents:** place beside the Seat's **battlefield-facing inward edge**.
- **Local player:** place the card **immediately above the Local Dock**, on the battlefield side, not inside the private Hand or on top of Guidance/Confirm/Skip.
- Derive anchor rectangles from the actual DOM Seat/Dock identified by server player ID. Recompute safely for phone/wide/4–8 seat layouts and resize; when full size cannot fit, use contained compact layout or a small verified recent-card count. Never relocate a physical Seat to create space.
- For **ordinary Attack/Dodge and non-Duel interactions**, show the latest public card for each involved player, with at most one recent subdued predecessor as space allows; older cards may be summarized. **Exception: an active Duel retains the real Duel root and every committed Attack in full, grouped at their true author's Seat/Dock with topology-dependent orientation and a cross-player chronological sequence index (§8.1).** No latest-per-duelist replacement or `+N` substitute; never create a card without a genuine public commit.

### 2.3 Visible emphasis

- A current public card/recent newly parked card may have a restrained gold/red/blue outline based on *card or result emphasis*, not a route or link.
- Pending **Attack against the current viewer** gives the **existing Local Dock response/Guidance panel a red-accented border and readable instruction**; the entire screen should not become a blocking red modal. When the legal response ends, this border promptly retires.
- Target selection uses a normal selected Seat accent; settled outcomes belong adjacent to the **true affected player's Seat/Dock** as short labels. Colour always has accompanying readable text or an icon.
- **Hide the persistent DECK and DISCARD piles entirely from the normal battlefield (not merely shrink or move them).** Preserve real draw/discard data, card lifecycle, event animation destinations and any dedicated legal pile interaction; see §8.2.

## 3. The one public-card presentation lifecycle

For each newly **server-confirmed, visible public played-card event**:

1. **REVEAL — user-approved policy B, 2026-10-14:** A server-committed real CardFace is shown at readable Hand size in the centre for **4,000ms from its first visible large painted frame by default**. **Exception: a NEW authoritative, committed public response/counter card belonging to the active interaction may interrupt this display early**, taking centre focus immediately. It then receives its own default 4s clock, which may likewise be interrupted by an even newer committed response. Polling, phase/CurrentAction changes, speculative/failed/private choices, timeout/Pass and a mere settlement message cannot interrupt. Supersedes the older ~500–800ms timing. See §8.7.
2. **MOVE:** On normal 4s expiry, move/shrink that same CardFace over **380ms** to its true author's Seat/Dock. **On valid committed-response interruption**, start parking the older card immediately (380ms motion when practical, without delaying the NEW large card), while the newly accepted card takes centre focus right away. Avoid two simultaneous full-size centred cards, ghost duplicates and incorrect card ownership. Use no connecting stroke.
3. **PARK/WAIT:** For ordinary public reactions, keep the public thumbnail at the genuine author's Seat/Dock while waiting. **Duel exception:** preserve the full committed-card history *split by the actual author*, stacked vertically below top Seats, extended inward horizontally at left/right Seats, and horizontally above the local player's Dock (§8.1). Every card carries its real cross-player order index. Never show an uncommitted response. The Local Dock alone presents `CurrentAction`.
4. **RESPONSE:** A later genuinely committed public response gets its **own** centre reveal and then parks before **its own author**, independent of who is currently the viewer.
5. **SETTLEMENT:** Show a **server-proven** blocked/damage/negated/otherwise resolved result close to the affected real Seat/Dock. Initial result readability target **~2 seconds**; next independent proven action supersedes old display promptly. Keep the author cards long enough to understand the result; no animation can hold up gameplay.
6. **RETIRE:** Remove expired public cards, overlays and labels without a ghost copy or sudden reappearance. **An actual confirmed new response may supersede the current centre card under policy B;** merely receiving a room update or newly pending response must NOT. Maintain real history and server gameplay deadlines independently.

**Identity/dedupe requirements:** Use authoritative event/root/interaction/actor/card identities. Polling and harmless rerenders never restart OR prematurely finish a reveal. A newly observed genuine event may reveal once; historic events on reload/view-switch reconstruct parked truth. **Policy B: never queue a newly committed response behind an earlier card's remaining four seconds.** The new verified response takes centre priority, and earlier cards park at their real owners. If a poll contains multiple committed events, respect verified causal order, keep all physical identities, and let the latest eligible response take centre; never create fake cards or block game decisions.

**Reduced motion:** Do not translate/scale animate. Show a readable, full-size static centre card for **up to four seconds**, shortened only by a subsequent genuine committed response under Policy B, then switch to the true Seat/Dock without travel. Preserve accessible announcements without duplicates.

**Zero graph elements:** The new public card presenter must never render SVG relationship connectors, coloured authored/effect strokes, arrows, line branches or graph-only card nodes. Implement geometry for *positioning moving card sprites*, not for representing relationships.

## 4. Authoritative reference flows

### 4.1 Ordinary Attack → Dodge from both viewpoints

**State A: Local attacker commits Attack.** Before Confirm, only the attacker's private selected Hand card/target selection is visible. After authoritative submission, show the real Attack **centre at Hand size** to viewers for whom it is public; message `Cao Cao used Attack on Zhao Yun`. After its normal 4s or a genuine new committed response interrupt (§8.7), shrink/park it **above Cao Cao's Dock** in Cao Cao's view, or **below Cao Cao's real opponent Seat** in other players' views. The legitimate target and responder are not deduced by watching the animation.

**State B: Defender sees incoming Attack.** In Zhao Yun's viewpoint, the newly visible genuine Attack first appears **in the centre**, accompanied by `Cao Cao used Attack on you`. It parks when the default hold ends or a real committed response interrupts, at **Cao Cao's true opponent Seat**, not at Zhao Yun. Zhao Yun's existing Local Dock response/Guidance acquires a red accented border and a clear short message, e.g. `Cao Cao attacked you. Play Dodge or Skip.` Show the real response timer and only the options offered by `CurrentAction`. A normal Attack is **not** generally playable as Dodge; physical Attack `AS DODGE` (Zhao Yun Braveheart/Longdan) appears only where the server genuinely allows conversion. Do not reveal a privately selected Dodge or invent a Dodge placeholder.

**State C: Defender confirms Dodge.** After server confirmation, the newly committed Dodge immediately supersedes any still-revealing Attack (§8.7). The publicly played genuine Dodge (or an actually proven physical Attack-as-Dodge with correct displayed physical art and played-as label) is shown **centre-first at Hand size** for each viewer. Message `Zhao Yun played Dodge`. Then shrink/park it **above Zhao Yun's Dock** in Zhao Yun's own view, or **beneath Zhao Yun's Hero Seat** in Cao Cao's and observers' views. Keep the older Attack thumbnail at Cao Cao's seat as subdued context until the blocked result is readable. No counter-effect line.

**State D1: Attack blocked.** When verified, show `DODGED` / `Attack blocked` at the true defender seat; leave recent Attack/Dodge thumbnails readable briefly, then retire. Never show `TAKE DAMAGE` simultaneously with a proved successful Dodge.

**State D2: Defender Skip/timeout/no legal Dodge.** Skip itself is **not** proof that damage happened. When server proof establishes real Attack damage, show `TAKE DAMAGE` adjacent to Zhao Yun's actual Seat/Dock (and accurate real HP state). Maintain a readable ~2s result, visible to attacker/defender/observer, even if the response round advanced quickly. If prevention/redirect or a special skill changes damage, announce only the actually proven outcome. Do not display a fictitious physical response card.

**All perspectives:** The same true card/actor/result identities must match; placement is different because physical Seats rotate for the viewer. The Local Dock highlight is **only** for the viewer with an actual pending decision. A spectator does not see or activate the defender's legal private Hand.

### 4.2 Duel

- Genuine publicly committed Duel stratagem: **Policy-B centre reveal (normally four seconds, interruptible by a new committed response)** then **first persistent card under/along the real Duel author's Seat/Dock**. During the shared Negation opportunity the genuine root Duel stays visible; uncommitted Negation intents are private. Do not display gigantic Duel/EFFECT/NEGATION panels.
- Once DUEL_EXCHANGE is authoritative, each genuinely committed Attack gets its own **Policy-B centre reveal**, which may interrupt the previous card, then **appends after that same author's previous cards** in their own anchored history. All players see actual server-proven card author and card identity, never a guessed alternation; Dodge cannot answer Duel.
- **Direction by physical seat topology (§8.1):** top-row duelist → cards vertically beneath their Hero; left-side duelist → horizontally inward to the right; right-side duelist → horizontally inward to the left; local duelist → horizontally across the battlefield immediately above the Local Dock. The spectator's screen can therefore contain **two distinct seat-attached Duel histories** simultaneously. No single global bottom Dock rail.
- **Chronology across both participants:** The actual root Duel is event #1; each subsequently confirmed Attack is #2, #3, #4, etc. Add compact globally unique order badges to the physical CardFaces so that a spectator knows whether Cao Cao or Zhen Ji answered next even when their cards are in separate columns. Never replace a previous card by its author or collapse history into latest-only +N.
- Histories are locally scrollable in their **own orientation** when many cards exceed safe battlefield space, never over hero portraits, other seats, response timer, central reveal, Hand, Guidance or Confirm/Skip. Keep proven settlement visible ~2s before orderly retirement. No second public CardFace, fabricated pending Attack, connector/graph or reopened root reveal on refresh.
### 4.3 Negation and counter-Negation

- With an open Negation opportunity but **no committed Negation**, show **only the original public action's card** in its genuine current centre-reveal or actor-parked phase and one compact public effect sentence; no provisional Negation CardFace, no private responder leak, no verbose `NEGATION RESPONSE`, `EFFECT` or `REACTION CHAIN` panel.
- **Exactly one current genuine card occupies the large central reveal slot at a time;** each newly confirmed Negation/counter-Negation may interrupt the previous centre card (§8.7), and the previous physical card parks under/alongside its **actual author's Seat** (or true local Dock), **never grouped with all responses in a central row**. The new response then reveals alone at centre before itself parking under its own Hero; the most recent public response is strongest. **Each still-visible genuine physical card gets its global within-chain ordinal (#1 root, #2 onward real responses), including repeated responses by the same author, per new §10.7.2.1 R2-D.** Older same-interaction cards remain subdued/compact and a history count may replace overflow **only when permitted without losing the ability to determine real physical card order**, and the one narration band remains stable near battlefield centre (§10.8.4 H4-B).
- Only server-proof outcome decides `effect negated`, `effect restored` or continuation. Preserve per-viewer private Local Dock's `Play Negation or Pass` (or `Pass` only) options and one shared, server-authoritative window deadline. Never infer parity of the public counter chain to change game rules.

### 4.4 Group/AOE and other public cards

- Root Barbarian Invasion, Raining Arrows, Oath etc. genuine public played card: **Policy-B centre reveal** → source's owner-side thumbnail. Subsequent *actually committed* Attack/Dodge/Negation from eligible participants reveal individually and park before each true author. Compact per-player `waiting / responded / avoided / damaged` information must be backed by real public statuses; no imaginary responses or per-target connectors.
- For a current Raining Arrows defender, use the authoritative Dodge providers; keep unrelated Hand cards visible but subdued. If no legal Dodge is available, the explicit local choice may be `TAKE DAMAGE` (per server-projected legal action), **not** a meaningless disabled Confirm. Numeric damage is never guessed.
- Genuine event-specific card displays (equipment, Judgment, Private Draw, Bumper Harvest, theft/discard selection, Hero skill continuations) keep their own rules in §5. No fabricated CardFace for skills without a real publicly played physical card. Generic skill notices remain small and nonblocking.

## 5. Existing UX2 functional designs that remain approved

These are **current functional requirements**, not old combat-graph tasks. They remain in force during both migration phases.

### 5.1 Hero Skills band (old §1)

- The Local Hero's Skills area next to the portrait is the **single activation entry point** for the viewer's actionable Hero skills. Fill available width: one skill uses full Skills band; two peer skills use equal width and equal height; names wrap naturally (prefer one line for Cao Cao's `TREACHERY` / `ENTOURAGE` when room permits). Touch targets at least **44×44 CSS px**; no clipped text or character-by-character wrapping.
- Skills requiring legal card/target/card-position selection launch the existing authoritative selection flow; generic Confirm/Cancel may remain in the Action Row, but **do not duplicate `Use <Hero Skill>` there**. Passives are clearly non-actionable; optional triggers become actionable only from server-projected choice.
- A *different* Hero's passive response choice offered to this viewer is a Local Dock decision, **not** an activation button for the viewer's Hero. Retain provider IDs, revisions, selection reset, privacy and all Standard roster reachability.

### 5.2 Zhuge Liang skills and private Stargazing (old §4)

- Actionable **Stargazing** is distinguishable from passive **Empty Fortress Strategem**; no duplicate generic action skill button. Private Stargazing has **three vertically ordered zones**: `TOP OF DECK`, `REVEALED CARDS`, `BOTTOM OF DECK`.
- Four initially private revealed CardFaces begin in the middle zone; Top and Bottom begin empty but remain usable drop zones. **Whole-card (not grip-only) touch-hold/pointer drag** supports assignment, moving between zones and reordering **within** a zone; visible insertion indicator, no duplicated/lost card, cancelled drag leaves prior state, normal scrolling when not dragging. **User-reported current grip-only touch limitation is explicitly targeted by §10.11 G1/G2; do not claim the existing code already meets this requirement.** On a ~390px phone show the four recognisable cards together when feasible; compact zones, contained overflow when necessary, no huge three-buttons-per-card grid.
- `Top` left→right is next draws first; `Bottom` left→right is placed after the remaining deck. Local lists `unassigned`, `top`, `bottom` each contain each card ID **exactly once**. `COMPLETE STARGAZING` is enabled only when `unassigned` is empty and authoritative `minTop/maxTop` constraints are satisfied; **all-to-Top or all-to-Bottom is legal if allowed**. Existing submission contract remains `{providerId:"private_deck_reorder",topCardIds,bottomCardIds}`. Keep server order, revision/busy guards, private-only visibility and keyboard/screen-reader movement alternative.

### 5.3 Response guidance, timer and System Menu (old §§3, 4A)

- The Local Guidance area describes the **actual next decision** (`Play Dodge or Skip`, `Play Negation or Pass/Skip` using the actual server-authorized label, `Take Damage` when the authoritative alternative applies), rather than implementation jargon like `Group Resolution`. Only real legal options are emphasized; unrelated Hand stays visible, subdued if appropriate. No guessed damage amounts.
- Response/event timers share a **compact lower-right battlefield cluster** just above Guidance: numeric timer typically ~52–68×36–44px for response or ~44–64×36–44px for event, separated from the ≥44×44px System Menu button by about 8px. Do not keep a second big timer at the top of the combat screen; preserve exact server deadline, privacy and visible-at rules.
- System Menu remains compact and contains **build SHA, Download UX trace and Exit Game**; no large diagnostics essay, preserve privacy filtering, JSON export, exit confirmation and useful short download feedback.
- When an expired or unavailable room returns to the entry screen, clear the saved room session, explain why the game ended, and keep any tab-local UX trace downloadable there; the render-error recovery screen must also retain trace export. Explicit Exit Game clears the saved session so reload does not restore the departed room.
- **Private Draw display (owner update 2026-10-30 23:01 UTC; R6-QA-03):** The actual newly received CardFace(s) remain visible **only to their authenticated owner** in the short existing private reveal, then enter that player's Hand. **Do not show the decorative `PRIVATE DRAW` / `ONLY YOU CAN SEE THESE CARDS` title/explanation box** above those cards. Maintain a private screen-reader region label (e.g. `Private drawn cards`) that is not a visible banner, retain the existing reveal duration, event timer, Hand update, inspect/accessibility behavior, privacy gating and content-driven layout without a large empty Stage. This later owner decision supersedes this section's former requirement for a visible `ONLY YOU CAN SEE THESE CARDS` label. Bumper Harvest and other event-specific timers continue to share the compact cluster; never leak card identity to another viewer.
- **Public skill outcome presentation — owner-approved R6-QA-02 (2026-10-30 23:12 UTC):** Every server-committed, publicly authorized skill result is shown through **one canonical H4/public-event narration**, whether it has a real physical CardFace or not; **no duplicate upper-right `EFFECT TRIGGERED` / `effectNotice` toast** for that same result. The one public H4 shares authoritative event identity/order, stage-centered collision-safe geometry, lifespan and accessible announcement. Do not invent a public CardFace for a no-physical-card skill result, derive a gained card name from private Hand, or guess damage from HP changes/adjacent events. PAC independently shows only the next public action; Console only authorized viewer choices; genuine private draw cards remain owner-private. A separate small system/non-outcome transient notice may remain only if it has an independently justified purpose and does **not** repeat a committed H4 result. This replaces the older blanket generic skill-result-toast policy, not the separate game rules or actual timers.

### 5.4 Unified compact player Inspect (old §4B.12)

- **Exactly one** compact public-information inspector. Selecting a legal opponent or using their Inspect affordance opens/updates the **same panel**, never `PREVIEW TARGET` + separate overlapped `INSPECT`. Changing the *inspection subject* alone must not silently change the legally selected gameplay target; clicking a legal target Seat may explicitly select it. Closing Inspect preserves an existing valid target/Confirm state.
- iPhone 17 Pro Max-style black/gold **upper/middle battlefield floating panel**, not full screen: use ~88–94% of usable safe width where feasible and content-driven height, roughly 40–50% battlefield-height cap with **internal scrolling** for overflow. Do not cover opponent Seats, Hand, Guidance or Confirm/Cancel; one close × control, no `View Details` button and no automatic full detail screen.
- Show name, recognisable Hero portrait, public HP, Hand **count** (not card identities), named public skills, actual public Equipment Weapon/Armour/+1/−1 horse, and public Judgment cards or `None`. Any hidden role remains hidden. **Tap a skill to show its canonical NAME and description in the shared Details region**; tapping another skill or visible public equipment/Judgement card switches the description directly. **Tapping the already selected item does not collapse/clear it (§8.5)**; informational only, never skill activation. No invented equipment or hand-back identities.
- Use actual server public data; compare equipment-rich real 4-player/6–8-seat cases and long skill descriptions, ensuring no overlapping panels or disabled local controls. **Inspector must keep long descriptions scrollable; tapping public equipment or another skill directly switches its named detail in the same open Inspector, with NO nested Back; inspected Hero and any legitimate selected target remain (§8.3–§8.5).** Equipment mini-card rank/suit/art must scale together.

### 5.5 Separate authoritative target-card picker (old §4C)

- The **interactive** card-selection modal is distinct from passive Inspect. Used for genuine server-offered options, e.g. **Retaliation (Sima Yi), Dismantle/Burning Bridge, Steal, Frost Sword, Kirin Bow**, and only other effects that expose real selectable keys.
- One shared black/gold **nonmodal battlefield-contained** panel with clear effect title, authenticated legal zone options, selection count, and legal action footer. **2026-10-18 §10.17 owner decision supersedes old full-screen modal and generic Cancel:** tap currently selected card to deselect; no standalone Cancel/Clear by default; only truly server-legal Skip or distinctly authorized abort if proven. Zone order **HAND → EQUIPMENT → JUDGMENT**; omit inapplicable empty zones. Preserve gold border/check, accessible non-colour selected indicator, ≥44px actions, and legitimate hidden Hand privacy.
- **Opponent Hand anonymity is absolute:** when server provides opaque `hand:0`, `hand:1` etc, show separate selectable **face-down** positions (normally all four visible together on 390px where feasible); never reveal rank, suit, art or inferred identity. When only a *single Hand-zone key* is provided, show a grouped/random Hand-zone fallback; **never invent individual choices**. Larger Hands use contained scroll/controlled overlap, not tiny slivers.
- Actual public Equipment and Judgment card positions appear face-up as eligible separate selections. Mixed-zone cases must work, including Hand + Equipment + Judgment and Frost Sword selection of **1–2 cards when that is the authoritative min/max**. Only exact legal keys can be selected; selection clears on action revision or target change.
- The in-battlefield decision panel is the **single** owner of Confirm/Use for `Retaliation / Burning Bridges / Steal / Frost Sword / Kirin Bow` etc; never duplicate in Local Dock. A second tap on the same pending card toggles OFF. **No generic Cancel or Clear button** just to deselect; only a distinct proven server-approved overall abort could justify Cancel, whereas a real optional `Skip` stands alone as the one negative action. Do not show Skip and redundant Cancel together. Mandatory choice has Confirm only, with re-tap to clear draft. Do not invent selection for random/forced effects; keep local legality, hidden Hand anonymity and authorization unchanged.
- At 390×844, ~440px, 480×900 and wide, prove actual mixed zone geometry, all four anonymous positions, selection clear, action buttons, no page overflow and no click-through.

## 6. Visual quality and production-path acceptance

**Mandatory functional checks (no fixture-only completion claims):** genuine server-backed four-player game, with authorized host-controlled test players when switching is needed. Test at 390×844, **~440px CSS iPhone 17 Pro Max**, 480×900, one wide viewport, and 4/6/8 Seat topology where relevant.

**New visual invariants:**
- **Zero** source-target/card-card relationship strokes, SVG arrows, old connector portals, historical route lines or per-player branch visualization.
- **Zero** old giant `EFFECT / NEGATION RESPONSE / REACTION CHAIN / Duel Exchange metadata` panels over normal gameplay.
- One centre public CardFace during each legitimate reveal; no second legacy copy. A truthful per-player thumbnail appears only when that author really played a public card.
- Real viewer-specific top Seats/Local Dock do not move. The public card for a given actor parks before that actor in **all** views; no player-to-card identity mismatch.
- Legal controls, visible response timer, touchable Hand, Inspect, skill buttons and System Menu continue functioning while cards reveal/park. No page horizontal scrolling, Seat/Guidance/Dock overlap or clipped card.
- Skip and timeout never generate fictitious Dodge or unsupported damage. Real blocked/Damage outcome visible near actual target long enough to read.
- Fast response/poll/refresh/test-view switch/reduced motion cannot repeat or leak a historical reveal. Public presentation never reveals hidden Hand identities or a private responder.

**Evidence grading:** Rule/API pass → public projection pass → browser fixture pass → real authenticated browser/game path pass → deployed device screenshot → **user visual acceptance**. CI or a mounted DOM element alone never proves the final UX. Capture actual visible screenshot and IDs/build SHA; report unsupported proof/fallback honestly.

**CI policy:** normal required push-to-CI-to-Cloudflare deployment/smoke target **≤6:00 wall clock**. Retain small meaningful build/typecheck, lint, rules/API/privacy and startup smoke; do not add large unapproved visual matrices to mandatory push CI. Heavy display/4–8-seat tests run focused/opt-in until user approves them. Do not suppress real errors or remove real safety coverage merely to pass CI.

## 7. Historical baseline migration — ARCHIVE ONLY (DO NOT EXECUTE)

**Completed previous work:** UX2-NO-GRAPH-REMOVAL-01 removed the old visual relation/graph UI and introduced a truthful, playable static public card view. UX2-CENTRE-REVEAL-SEAT-CARDS-02 then introduced proof-backed centre CardFace reveal and true actor-seat parking, beginning with Attack/Dodge. Both are historic migrations; **do not rerun, restore the old UI, or use old migration acceptance timing**.

**Current visual authority:** §§1–6, **§8.7 Policy B** and family-specific accepted sections. The initial migration's old static presenter, approximate 500–800ms read, 250–450ms travel, historic Step D sequencing and graph-specific source observations are **superseded**, not future work. Previous detailed implementation and CI history is available through Git history, not active task instructions.

**Next work:** Follow the current execution index at the top. Coding Agent owns `HANDOVER.md`; pending Reviewer acceptance does not block another authorized UX2 task.

## 8. PHASE 1 — Existing accepted UX baseline; §8.7 deployed pending phone review

**Historical user evidence (earlier defects, since addressed under §8.1–§8.4):** Four UX defects observed on an actual ~440 CSS px iPhone WTK game: (1) the root Duel card/earlier Attack disappeared after later Duel Attack; (2) permanent central DECK / DISCARD blocks are unwanted; (3) a public hero's skill rules are clipped when the Inspect panel contains public equipment; mini equipment card rank/suit corners visually dominate; (4) tapping public equipment inside Inspect closes the entire hero panel, opens a large separate card explanation, and closing the explanation does not return to Inspect. Attached user screenshots showed a genuine Duel Exchange with two Attack cards, Hua Tuo's equipment-rich Inspector, and the oversized Sky Piercing Halberd description. Supplied iPhone UX trace: build SHA 064b30801cd11b5dad523c82599a95e123e39175, viewport 440×894; it records proven DUEL_EXCHANGE and Duel/Attack public events; it does **not** itself prove why every missing card unmounted. These were the *original* baseline defect reports; see current acceptance status below, not a new instruction to reproduce already accepted work.

**Historical 2026-10-14 pre-fix source inspection, NOT a current-code assertion:** app/public-card-display.tsx: latestDuelEventsByActor(), completeDuelJourneyEvent() filters by actorId, and DuelCardJourney initially parks the latest by actor; this intentionally loses earlier cards. app/page.tsx: InteractionStage calls onCloseInspect before onInfoCard when the opponent's public card is clicked, while a separate global card-info-backdrop mounts for infoCard. app/sequence-overrides.css limits Inspector shell to min(53cqh, 400px), clips overflow, and limits the nested skill description to 5 lines of height with its own scrolling; extra equipment can crowd out visible context. app/page.tsx still renders draw-stack and discard-stack in play-center despite the prior §2.3 instruction. Keep current §8.1 per-actor Duel history, and do not change approved §9 Negation rules. **§8.7 Policy B is the newest presentation authority.**

### 8.1 Duel — complete chronological histories anchored to each actual Seat/Dock (seat-aware)

**User confirmation (2026-10-14):** The user confirms the current viewer-relative Duel layout has passed validation. This accepts the reviewed Duel layout only; other UX checkpoints remain subject to their own review.

**2026-10-14 screenshot correction (overrides previous full bottom-rail design):** A real iPhone screenshot of Cao Cao and Zhen Ji in the **top row** shows their existing one-card-under-seat thumbnails and +1 history marker. For a multi-round Duel, keep **all committed cards** under/next to the respective actual card author's Hero, but choose vertical or horizontal progression from **the viewer-relative physical Seat layout**. **Do not collect all players' Duel cards in one bottom rail or place another player's card over the local player's Dock.** This is a seat-attached ordered layout, not a source-target line/graph.

**Single authoritative shared history, multiple physical containers:** Maintain one chronological server-proven list of physical committed cards for the active Duel (root Duel event followed by all legitimate Attack-response events), sorted by authoritative event sequence / proof. For rendering, partition that SAME list by **actual actorId**, preserving relative order per actor. Each event occupies one CardFace at that actor's true Seat/Dock; each carries the **global ordinal** (1 for root Duel, 2/3/4/… for responses) so a spectator can reconstruct the alternating chronology across both players without arrows. Use authentic art/suit/rank and optionally a restrained actor label; never misattribute source/target or show a privately selected/failed/noncommitted response.

**Seat geometry and progression (based on measured DOM anchors, not fixed player numbers or static clockwise positions):**

| Viewer-relative actual location of the player who committed the card | History container and progressive direction |
| --- | --- |
| **Top-row opponent Hero** (like screenshot Cao Cao / Zhen Ji) | **Vertical column downward**, beginning immediately **below** the player's real hero card. Cards appear top → bottom in that player's commit order. Do not fan outward horizontally into another top Seat. |
| **Left-side opponent Hero** (6–8-seat side layout, etc.) | **Horizontal row to the right, toward central battlefield**, starting just past this actual Hero's inner/right edge; show oldest near the Hero and append newer cards to the right. |
| **Right-side opponent Hero** | **Horizontal row toward central battlefield to the left**, starting at the Hero's inner/left edge. Oldest nearest that Hero, later cards progressively farther left; their leftward visual order must remain unambiguous via ordinal badges and correct direction. |
| **Local player / bottom Dock** | **Horizontal row immediately above the local player's Dock**, within battlefield safe width; no overlap with hero, Hand, skill buttons, Guidance, response clock or Confirm/Skip. Root/Attack cards belong here **only when the local player truly committed them**. |
| **Any unusual 4–8-player or responsive configuration** | Classify from the measured current Seat/Dock rectangle and actual battlefield layout. Re-evaluate on viewport resize, Chrome/Safari toolbar changes, actual viewer switch and seat reflow; never assume opponent 2 is always top or player 1 is always left. |

**Motion and size:** Every newly **server-committed** public Duel or Attack gets the §8.7 Policy-B centre reveal with true actor/target text, then moves into its **next free slot** in the actor's own oriented history. Previously parked cards stay stable; they must **never disappear after a second Attack by the same actor**, be replaced by latestDuelEventsByActor, or become a +1-only count. Initial thumbnail target around **44×66 to 48×72 CSS px** at ~440px viewport, genuine 2:3 CardFace, and **small clear ordinal badge**, respecting mini rank/suit/card identity. The root Duel remains the first card of its author's group, and retains global ordinal #1.

**Safe clipping / long Duels:** Size each actor's history to its own collision-free rectangle (not merely screen width): top columns end before the centre action sentence/next occupied region and especially before local Dock; side rows end before the other column/centre action sentence; local row fits between battlefield controls. When 4/6/8 or more genuine responses exceed available extent, **keep every card in the data and use contained, independently scrollable overflow in that seat's reading direction** (vertical touch scroll below top Seats; horizontal touch scroll beside side Seats / above local Dock). Include visible scroll affordance and ordinal badges and an accessible count / event list; bring newest card into view when appended, allow user scroll back to #1 and older cards. Do not cause document-level overflow, overlap another Seat, clamp every card to the same coordinate, or substitute +N for the unseen genuine CardFaces. The active central reveal may temporarily float over the battlefield but never cover legal response controls; collision layout must take the revealed CardFace/notice, timer, Inspect and system cluster into account.

**Lifecycle, privacy and provenance:** Only server-proven Duel / Duel Attack response events are in this history, dedupe by interactionId + eventId and server order; no fake Pass, unsubmitted Attack, Dodge, hidden Hand item, or client-inferred parity. Preserve complete sequence across poll, refresh, reconnect, test viewer switch, reduced motion and animations; historical cards reconstruct directly in seat containers without replaying old centre reveals. A new independent Duel starts a new history; genuine settlement stays legible ~2s and then retires. Animation or scrolling must never extend Duel response deadlines or interfere with §9 Negation windows. Exactly one visual owner per event: disable competing TableResolutionSequence copy for the same committed card.

**Implementation status (2026-10-14):** The actor-keyed reduction and same-actor event filtering have been removed. `app/page.tsx` passes the validated server response ordinal to `app/public-card-display.tsx`, which retains each proven Duel event, partitions history by its real actor, and renders measured seat-oriented scroll tracks with global ordinals. Root and response cards remain backed by the existing validated public proof; basic Attack/Dodge and game rules are unchanged. Commit `15f0c0a53fb22bdb1a24bd79b3d995da68feb4f1` passed Actions run `38527792209`, including D1 migration, Worker deployment and production smoke test. The user confirmed on 2026-10-14 that the viewer-relative Duel layout passed validation.

**Acceptance cases (real authenticated multi-client):** Four-player **two top-row duelists**, root below true initiator and genuine Attack responses vertically stacked below each real author's top Seat; 5/6/8-player **left/right side-position duelists** with two inward horizontal histories; **local participant vs top or side** with bottom horizontal history only for self; source/target/viewer switch showing identical real event IDs and sequence ordinals, differing only in geometry. Test root + 1/2/3/6+ responses, repeated same actor 2–3 times, correct global numbering, overfull vertical/horizontal scroll, final settlement, fresh Duel reset, stale/duplicate polls, mid-Duel reload, mobile 390×844/~440×894/480×900 and desktop, reduced motion and no overlay collisions. A screen with all cards in one bottom rail, vanished earlier cards, or only latest-per-player +1 history FAILS.
### 8.2 Remove normal battlefield DECK and DISCARD piles

**ACCEPTED (user, 2026-10-14):** Persistent DECK/DISCARD piles are hidden. Deployed build 4a3d93f35153f21ece8bf116a519e7b8f7489276; prior CI/deploy passed. Do not implement this again. Preserve its nonvisual card movement anchors and real game rules.

- Neither **84 / DECK** nor a permanently face-up **DISCARD** pile should be visible on the regular playing battlefield, on phone **or desktop**. Remove their visible frames, labels, top discarded face and reserved visual block rather than quietly scaling them down or moving them elsewhere. Recover collision-free battlefield space for top-Seat vertical and side-Seat horizontal Duel histories, the local horizontal history, and centre reveals.
- **Presentation-only change:** retain server deckCount, discardTop, D1 deck/discard/card conservation, drawing, discard/judgement animation semantics, ability/cost resolution and any actual legal card-selection zones. Keep *nonvisual* positioning anchors if animations depend on data-draw-anchor/data-discard-anchor and measured rectangles; use a tiny invisible/pointer-events-none geometry target or replace destination safely. Avoid display:none on an anchor still requiring measurable coordinates. A publicly played card may still briefly animate out on a genuine discard event, but must not park as a permanent discard pile.
- Ensure removing visible stacks does not accidentally hide the **different** public Bumper Harvest reveal, private Draw, Judgement and Stargazing deck-reorder controls, discard-phase selection, or a proof-backed card currently in motion.

### 8.3 Inspect panel — always-readable hero skills with equipped cards

**ACCEPTED (user, 2026-10-14):** Inspector shell/content size and long skill-description scrolling. Implementation delivered at 81d015fd85408ac9e74ecc19fb23020aee24ef79; CI/deploy passed. Preserve this geometry; do not redo it.

- The Inspector's **header + Hero portrait/name/HP/Hand count/public skill buttons + equipment/judgement** are a compact stable summary. Reserve enough vertical space for an explicit **DETAILS area with independent vertical scroll** below the summary, or make a single predictable content scroll region under the fixed header. Tapping First Aid / Prodigal Healer (and any other long skill) must show the **complete canonical description**, accessible by touch scroll, not clipped by overflow:hidden or hidden beneath the bottom Dock. The user should be able to notice that more text is scrollable.
- The Inspector must fit **inside the battlefield safe zone** above the Local Dock and out of the opponent Seats, Guidance, Hand and action row, including iPhone Chrome/Safari 390×844, 440×894, 480×900 and desktop; use measured available height rather than hard-coded 53cqh alone. Do not enlarge the panel over critical controls or allow a second page scrollbar. When equipment appears (1→2→3→4 cards), collapse/tighten public equipment layout and **keep skill description space usable**; overflow in equipment rows must be managed locally rather than pushing Details out of view.
- Keep public equipment miniature rank/suit/art and genuine 2:3 proportion. **User-approved §8.5 supersedes the requirement to retain tiny visible name/category text on an Inspector equipment thumbnail:** hide those miniature words and make suit/rank readable, while keeping complete accessible names and canonical card details in the shared pane.
- Preserve existing close control, current legal target, focus/accessibility and information privacy. A skill button opens only a description; it must never trigger that Hero's real gameplay skill.

### 8.4 Inspect public equipment detail — in-panel, Hero state preserved (ACCEPTED)

**ACCEPTED (user, 2026-10-14):** Public equipment detail stays inside Inspector, without destroying Hero context. Delivered in b1c06696ed4d19c54b6adaa3ff628b89148062c1 with lint fix 53460f685a482260d82e9d550dd13f84ea25b35f. **§8.5's newer one-tap switching supersedes any visible Back control and is now user-accepted.**

- Tapping a visible public equipped CardFace (or public Judgement CardFace) **must not invoke onCloseInspect before showing information**. Keep the same player's Inspector mounted and retain its active hero, expanded skill, currently selected target, and scroll state. Avoid opening a screen-filling global PRIVATE CARD INFORMATION dialog for an opponent's already-public equipment.
- Keep an **inline named explanation** inside the same Inspector Details pane with the Hero summary visible. **The previous nested Back/Exit button is superseded by §8.5:** touching another public equipment, Judgement card or skill instantly replaces the detail. The Inspector top-right × remains the only visible close control; no secondary modal is introduced.
- Keep the existing generic card-info dialog where appropriate for a *local Hand card*, but its label must not falsely describe public equipped cards as private. Switching the shared Details selection must not alter a selected combat target, submitted action, Hand selection, or current server CurrentAction. Preserve keyboard Escape as a fallback and sensible focus on the selected skill/equipment; **do not add a visible nested Back or Exit control (§8.5)**.
- Switching from Hero A to Hero B intentionally updates Inspector subject and resets only subject-dependent nested detail; merely switching from skill to equipment and back within the **same** Hero does not lose context.

### 8.5 Public equipment miniatures and skill/equipment details — USER-ACCEPTED / CLOSED

**Accepted UX (D1/D2):** Opponent Equipment uses small recognizable genuine art and conspicuous rank/suit without miniature printed card-face text. A single titled nonmodal Inspector switches instantly between real named Hero skills and public Equipment details, preserving the selected Hero's state and real card ownership. No nested Back/modal chain, duplicate pane, hidden-card leak or invented equipment. Earlier D1/D2 delivery and visual layout experiments are historical and not new work.

### 8.6 Historical Phase-1 delivery summary — ARCHIVE ONLY (DO NOT EXECUTE)

**Checkpoint A (Duel full history):** delivered, documented in §8.1 and **user visually validated 2026-10-14**. Prior actor-keyed collapse is retired. Do not rebuild the old global bottom rail or latest-per-player +N.

**Checkpoint B (hide Deck/Discard):** delivered at commit 4a3d93f35153f21ece8bf116a519e7b8f7489276 and **user accepted**. Visible central piles stay absent; invisible geometry anchors/card movement continue working.

**Checkpoint C (Inspector layout and nested public card info):** delivered by b1c06696ed4d19c54b6adaa3ff628b89148062c1 plus lint fix 53460f685a482260d82e9d550dd13f84ea25b35f, and **user accepted as §§8.3–8.4**. Later §8.5 removed the nested Back and introduced direct titled switching. No old Back-based test or reimplementation is authorized.

**Historical phone review before R1 delivery:** §8.7 default 4s centre hold and true-author parking passed, but a genuine legal defender response was observed blocked during the visual hold. §10.7 R1 subsequently delivered a repair (PENDING REVIEW). Preserve response deadlines and legal server decisions.

### 8.7 Reveal Policy B — implemented/deployed; real-phone timing review pending (2026-10-14)

**Coding-agent progress (2026-10-14):** Policy-B implementation was pushed as `871f3071056c21aeb1b1c961276d54115a888a22`; Actions run `38603216133` passed Lint/fast tests, API tests, browser startup/room smoke, and deploy. Production `/api/health` returned `{"ok":true,"worker":"available"}`. Real-device visual acceptance remains pending.

**Latest user design confirmation, overriding the previous queued-all-cards approach:** **Policy B** is final. Newly committed public physical cards normally remain large and central for **4 seconds**. However, when an **actual subsequent public response/counter card is server-confirmed**, it may **interrupt the earlier large-card presentation immediately**, move the older genuine card to its own author area, and give the newer committed card the centre with a **fresh default 4-second reveal**. If a third legitimate public response arrives, it can likewise supersede the second card before its own 4 seconds end. A 4-second hold is therefore **conditional on no genuinely committed next response**, not guaranteed uninterrupted for every card.

**Original P0 report and remaining acceptance test (not proof of a defect in the later deployed fix):** In the user's earlier iPhone test, Attack appeared large for less than one second. Under policy B, this is **correct ONLY if** a different, newly *committed* public response/counter card actually arrived and took its place. Shrinking merely because response mode opened, a defender got CurrentAction, polling refreshed the room, a timer started, a player clicked a privately selected card, a Pass/timeout occurred, or a settlement message appeared is WRONG. Investigate which happened in the real scenario before claiming bug fixed. The original Attack/other card's author, physical ID, chronology and settlement must stay authoritative.

**Reviewed code risk checklist (hypotheses, not proven causal attribution):** A prior code fix was committed as b1edbfe7d50e759c7bf5ef4888e0f0119a0f1a59 **before** Policy B was confirmed; inspect its queued-event lifecycle and do not assume it satisfies the later interruption decision.
- app/public-card-display.tsx defines 4,000ms hold and 380ms move, but AttackDodgeCardJourney's moveRootOutOfCenter may advance Attack on response existence. This is **permitted only when the response is a validated newly committed public physical card** and that card immediately receives centre focus. It must not advance on a pending response or projection re-render.
- app/page.tsx currently marks publicCardJourneySeen at first ready mount rather than after animation completion; if presenter temporarily disables or remounts, an unfinished card may return parked even though no newer committed card displaced it. Check owner lifecycle, enabled gating by Inspector/pickers, scope switching and legacy TableResolutionSequence before coding.
- Attack/Dodge/Negation/Deflection reveal token currently uses 60×90 CSS px versus Duel 90×135 and parked 48×72. Measure real computed size beside a rendered local Hand CardFace at mobile widths; the user must clearly perceive a large **readable** card, not a near-miniature.

#### 8.7.1 Precise display and interruption contract

1. **Uninterrupted path:** A newly seen, server-committed real card is painted at true readable Hand-card-scale at centre, records a first-painted timestamp (performance-based on viewer), and remains full-size until approximately t=4,000ms. At t=0.5, 2.5 and 3.5 seconds it still has full centre dimensions. At approximately t=4s, animate that physical card to the genuine actor's Seat/Dock over 380ms and park. There must be no <1-second automatic collapse with no subsequent committed public card.
2. **Interrupting path (explicitly accepted):** Example: Player A's Attack appears at t=0; player B's valid Dodge **commits** at t=0.5s. At the time of that commitment reaching this viewer, the Attack starts leaving centre for A's Seat/Dock without waiting until t=4; the genuine Dodge takes the centre immediately at full readable size with its **own 4s default hold**. The outgoing Attack may move in 380ms concurrently, but must not keep the new Dodge waiting 380ms or cause two overlapped full-size centre cards. If no further physical response commits, Dodge remains until 4s then moves to B. If a new verified response arrives sooner, it may interrupt Dodge in the same way.
3. **Eligibility to interrupt is strict:** Only a DIFFERENT, new server-accepted and publicly provable physical reaction/counter within the same active interaction qualifies: Dodge, Negation/counter-Negation, the legitimate next Duel Attack, a proven Deflection **cost CardFace** with skill name, and other explicitly implemented physical response events. A new unrelated root is a separate interaction retirement policy, NOT treated as an invented response. Mere server phase change, active responder assignment, pending/secret player choice, Pass/timeout, visual status notice, same event replay, duplicate poll, root target update or settlement with no newly committed physical response MUST NOT shorten the old centre card.
4. **No lost or duplicated cards:** On interruption, older card still parks with correct true author, remains as relevant interaction history and does not disappear. The new card gets its own centred CardFace; all proven events keep stable IDs and causal order. If several confirmations arrive within one server poll, use their authoritative commit order; prioritise the latest relevant committed card at centre while securely landing earlier real cards at their owners (do not animate an unbounded backlog or fabricate simultaneous public cards). Never move the Attack to the defender's Seat, or a Deflection cost to the original attacker's Seat.
5. **State and timing stability:** Mere component re-render, 1–2s polling, timer rerender, client CurrentAction revision or target highlight change must not alter the four-second countdown. Maintain viewer-session lifecycle keyed by real room/root/event identity, not by rebuilt object equality or premature "seen" flag. Late join/refresh may reconstruct historical cards parked without replaying old centre reveals; a genuinely active first reveal in the same viewer session should not be prematurely marked completed.
6. **Gameplay separation and accessibility:** Server responses, deadlines and shared 8s Negation windows proceed immediately regardless of visual read time. No animation blocks local Hand/Guidance/Confirm/Skip, Inspector or timer. Reduced-motion cards stay full-size statically until 4s or genuine new committed response, then switch to parked without travel. This presentation policy does not reveal uncommitted Negation intents or privately selected Hand costs.
7. **Scope:** Applies to proof-backed Attack/Dodge, Duel/Attack exchanges, single-target root/Negation chains, Da Qiao Deflection cost-card response, and the already shipped Dismantle root where applicable. Does NOT force 4s on private Draw, Hero Inspector, generic skill toasts or non-card settlement notices. Preserve all specialist legal flow, Duel's full seat-oriented history and approved public-card UI without graphs.

#### 8.7.2 Delivered repair requirements and remaining real-phone verification (historical P0)

**P0-A — Source-grounded diagnosis:** Reproduce the real-user Attack screenshot in an authenticated test room, from attacker/target/observer. Trace for each event: server-confirmed proof/event ID, first real central paint time, computed CardFace dimensions, phase change, outgoing/incoming actor, reason for interruption, seen status, enabled status, and any competing legacy presenter. Test Attack without Dodge, Attack followed by immediate 0.5-second real Dodge, Attack while defender only receives private response prompt, polling/Inspector toggles, and two physical committed Negation/counter cards. Distinguish legitimate policy-B interruption from accidental early shrink; report which caused the user's observed under-one-second case.

**P0-B — Minimal lifecycle repair:** Keep a default four-second viewer-session clock per newly painted public card. Wire **only newly committed public response/counter proofs** as interruption signals. If needed, correct moveRootOutOfCenter condition, premature publicCardJourneySeen, transient enabled/unmount, duplicate TableResolutionSequence event ownership and actor-seat anchoring. On valid interrupt park old card once while the new CardFace is immediately readable in centre. Keep fixed 380ms parking where motion is enabled; do not delay response controls or create extra fake CardFace. Preserve all other gameplay and server response timeout rules.

**P0-C — Two distinct acceptance cases with real timing and dimensions:**
- **No successor:** New Attack (or other root) stays visibly centred and full-size at t=0.5, 2.5 and 3.5 seconds, begins moving after around 4 seconds, and parks after around 380ms. Repeated polling/phase changes/Inspector should not shrink it in under one second.
- **Successor commits early:** Attack at t=0; authentic Dodge at t≈0.5s. Attack visibly leaves centre promptly, Dodge is visible in centre promptly **without a queued 4-second wait**; Dodge stays large until its own t≈4s if no third response. Verify that the earlier Attack is truly parked and both public actors/card IDs correct. Repeat with Duel response, Negation counter and Da Qiao Deflection. Never assert a full 4s for the intentionally interrupted Attack.
- For each, measure actual dimensions, phase times and evidence from real browser rendering; verify mobile 390×844, ~440×894, 480×900 and wide, reduced motion, rapid rerender/poll, identity dedupe and no response-control obstruction. Report actual smartphone recordings/screenshots and exact measured results. Fixture tests or timing constants alone do not validate screen experience.

**P0-D — Historical delivery and review evidence:** Prior CI/deploy was completed; exact-SHA real-phone visual acceptance evidence of both uninterrupted and genuine-interrupted presentation remains PENDING REVIEW. Record verified defects as bounded future fixes; **do not stop unrelated approved UX2 development awaiting human review**.

**Ownership:** Coding agent owns code, tests, deployment and HANDOVER.md. Planner/Reviewer owns only this design document and does not edit the handover.

## 9. PHASE 2 — Shared-window Negation (A–C and N0–N3 delivered; §10.7 R2 public-history fix pending review)

**UX2 Negation Plan A — latest confirmed user rule (2026-10-14):** Open one confidential shared window for **ALL living seats**, with immutable **maximum 8-second deadline**. Every player may explicitly submit a valid Negation intent or Pass (including those with no Negation card). **As soon as ALL living seats have persisted a response, the server may atomically settle the window early.** If even one living seat is silent, do NOT infer their Hand, auto-Pass or shorten the window; resolve at the original 8s deadline, treating the missing submission as timeout/implicit Pass then. In either case choose only one actually valid Negation by canonical seat order; open a fresh maximum-8s counter window after each actual winner. Legal Confirm/Pass is available from T0 regardless of root reveal; successful submissions are acknowledged privately, and only the chosen physical winning card is public after settlement. **No immediate-public-on-individual-Confirm (deferred UX3 §11).**

**Scope:** Rule/transport/privacy change only for Negation and counter-Negation (including Duel, ordinary Stratagem, judgement, Group/AOE per-target and Harvest target Negation). Do not change Attack/Dodge, Duel Attack exchange, rescue, triggers, other card legalities, public card presentation direction, or previously accepted no-graph UX. This is not an additional UX2 visual-migration task ID.

### 9.1 Deterministic window protocol

1. **Open:** When an effect may be Negated, create one server-authoritative confidential window for **ALL living seats**, irrespective of private cards/skill providers. Persist opaque windowId, canonical seat-priority start, ordered snapshot of the living seats, effect/root causal identity, openedAt, **immutable maximum deadline = openedAt + 8,000ms** and private submissions. Keep existing source/target starting anchors for ordinary Stratagem, Judgement, Group/AOE and Harvest. Everyone sees the same remaining clock until settlement; nobody sees others' cards, intent type, submitted seats or completion count.
2. **Offer in parallel to everyone:** All living players receive their own private CurrentAction. If the server finds any genuinely legal Negation card or Hero-skill/conversion provider, offer those legitimate options PLUS Pass; otherwise offer **Pass ONLY**. **Never automatically submit Pass just because the player lacks a provider.** The no-card player must explicitly tap Pass for the window to close early; doing nothing is allowed and becomes implicit Pass only at 8s timeout. Confirm stores a secret intent, not a public played card; unrelated actions remain disabled.
3. **Individual lock and early-finish eligibility:** A server-accepted Negation intent **or explicit Pass** is persisted and locked for that player's window; show only to them “Response submitted — waiting”. Duplicate identical submission stays idempotent, conflict rejects. **After each successfully persisted submission**, the server may check whether **every ID in the original living-seat snapshot** has a valid persisted response. If yes, claim/settle this window immediately without waiting for T0+8s. If any seat is missing, keep the original deadline; neither polling nor a client's guess may close it. **Never auto-submit Pass or remove no-card players from this check.** Do not publish completion counts, submitted IDs or individual choices.
4. **Close at the earlier of two authoritative conditions:** **(A)** every living seat in the window snapshot has **explicitly persisted Negation intent OR Pass**, or **(B)** the immutable T0+8s deadline is reached. The server, not React/polling, atomically claims settlement once and validates all accepted submissions; if condition A was reached at time T, do not accept late duplicate/conflicting writes after the claim. The actual winner is the first valid Negation by canonical seat order, **NEVER the fastest submitter**. Losing intents do not cost cards. If no valid winner, settle the underlying effect. Concurrent final submits, timer expiry and retries must not double-close, consume twice or skip a legitimate responder.
5. **Commit one physical card:** For the winning intent only, perform the usual authoritative card consumption (exactly once), Negation effect toggle, log/public card event, real actor identity and causal history. All losing intents are voided with **zero cards consumed**, even if they submitted earlier/later, and no fake card is revealed. Each player's own private final status can reflect whether their intent was used; other players see only the accepted public card. Ensure card conservation and existing hero provider legality.
6. **Counter window:** If a Negation wins, open a NEW shared 8-second window against that actual Negation. Seat-order priority starts with the **next living seat after the actual committed Negation's actor**, wrapping around; include every living seat (including the last actor upon wrap-around, if a legal provider still exists). All previous losing intents are discarded, **never automatically carried over** as counter-Negations, because the tactical decision/target is different. Repeat for counter-counter-Negation without arbitrary chain-depth cutoff; preserve genuine legal game termination and event proof.
7. **Final settlement:** When a window closes with no accepted Negation, settle the current negated/restored effect exactly once. A Duel must NEVER begin its Attack exchange until the root shared window **and every genuine counter window** complete. Preserve per-target cancellation semantics for multi-target effects.

### 9.2 Concrete seat example and priority

Five seats clockwise **1→2→3→4→5**; seat 1 plays Duel against seat 3.

- Shared window #1: all five living seats privately receive Negation/Pass; seats 2 and 4 submit actual Negation intents, seats 1, 3 and 5 **explicitly Pass**. When the **last** of these five submissions is persisted (possibly before 8s), the server settles early: **seat 2 wins** by seat priority, only seat 2's Negation becomes public, seat 4 retains their card. If seat 5 stays silent, the window instead remains open until 8s; the winner is still seat 2.
- Shared window #2: a fresh **up-to-8s** confidential counter window goes to ALL living seats, priority **3→4→5→1→2**. Seat 4 may independently submit a counter intent; it becomes a real public card **only when all five explicitly respond or the new 8s timer expires**, if chosen by priority. If seat 4 wins, Duel is restored and a separate window #3 starts at seat 5.
- After the first or any subsequent shared window that closes with no successful new Negation, resolve the current root effect state. Seat 3 starts the Duel Attack exchange **only if Duel ultimately remains active**.
- If only seat 4 submits Negation and all other seats **explicitly Pass**, seat 4 wins **immediately after the last response**. If others are silent, the server waits until 8s even if they lack Negation cards. If everyone Passes explicitly, settle early with no winner; if nobody responds, settle at 8s. No seat-by-seat 8s turns.

### 9.3 Time, privacy, transport and UX invariants

- Define **NEGATION_WINDOW_MS = 8_000** as the **MAXIMUM duration**, not a forced wait, for each confidential shared root/counter window. Preserve opensAt and its immutable maximum deadline; polling, refresh, animation, reconnect and submissions never move that deadline. **Only the server's proven all-seats-explicit-submission condition may settle early.** Show the real shared remaining countdown until actual settlement; do not retroactively rewrite deadlines. Keep existing **HUMAN_RESPONSE_TIMEOUT_MS = 60_000** and all unrelated response timers unchanged.
- **No leak through actor ownership:** This family is no longer modeled as a single pending actor for decision authorization. Introduce a window state (or compatible canonical pending extension) plus **per-viewer** private CurrentAction projection, not a single shared actorId that is reused by the UI. Only each owner sees legal Negation provider IDs, own selectable Hand, own submission status; nobody sees others' choices/Pass, card capabilities or intent IDs.
- On public projections scrub all uncommitted responder/seat signals, including **raw room JSON, pending, actionPlayerId, currentAction, causalEnvelope checkpoint/frame resolvingPlayerId, presentationV2, presentationSnapshot, logs, timeline, debug traces and test-viewer controls**. Distinguish a previously **committed public Negation actor** from confidential pending intent owners. Do not publish raw submittedIds, submitted count, queued card IDs, ranking or “only player X can answer”. The shared deadline, stage and public root effect/source/target may be shown, but not who has a legal card. Test **wire JSON from separate actual human tokens**, not only DOM.
- Existing compact battlefield Response Time cluster shows **one common 8s countdown** and optional short “Negation response window” copy. The Local Dock for a viewer with legal provider: “Play Negation or Pass”; a viewer with none: “No Negation available. Pass.”; a viewer who submitted: “Response submitted — waiting”. Do not show opponent choices. No second timer, big Negation stage, graph/connector, overlay that blocks Hand/Confirm/Skip, or pre-accepted centre CardFace.
- **Privacy invariant (previous optimistic-display risk already addressed in Gate C):** A private shared-window Negation intent MUST NOT create a public/speculative CardFace, discard movement or effect toggle; only the server-confirmed winner gets a public reveal. A new confirmed counter can immediately supersede the older centre card under §8.7 Policy B. Reject/ignore stale or losing intents without ghost animations.
- Server must validate **windowId + authenticated player identity + deadline + one-intent-per-seat + legal provider + authoritative selection**. Never trust a client-provided seat ranking or client timestamp. Concurrent writes must preserve independent players' intents without lost updates; do not allow one player's submission to invalidate another's stable window ID/revision. Use atomic conditional writes / a dedicated submissions table if needed; serialize close/commit/next-window atomically to prevent double consumption. Do not use an in-memory only window: state survives Cloudflare worker instances, refresh, restart and reconnect.
- Deadline `advance_timers` can settle at T0+8s. Additionally, **after a successful authenticated Negation intent or explicit Pass is persisted**, the SERVER checks that **every seat ID in the original living-seat snapshot** has a persisted response in the current window, and if so atomically settles early. No client inference or automatic Pass. A disconnected/silent/no-card player remains outstanding until an explicit response or 8s timeout; only timeout treats silence as implicit Pass. Prevent final-submission vs timer races, duplicate commits and stale-window writes. Avoid costly per-poll database scans; check via indexed durable submissions after writes.
- **Privacy tradeoff, explicitly accepted:** Early public settlement may signal that **all living players have finished responding**. It must NOT expose who held Negation, how anyone responded, submitted-player counts or choice order. Automatically filtering no-card seats would disclose capability through the timeline and is **forbidden**; an explicit Pass is required even for a Pass-only player. Keep individual submission private, strip per-seat state from public JSON/proof/debug and test actual authenticated observer/human tokens. Do not claim this aggregate completion-timing signal is cryptographically hidden.

### 9.4 Delivery and review status — COMPLETED CODE (not an execution queue)

**Gate A — complete, 2026-10-14:** durable shared 8s Negation window, simultaneous private intents and deterministic seat-priority winner/counter windows. Implementation 9f2bc6be007065f7b458f70e02964124386de28f and migration snapshot repair 1306d519afd6094200dfe2e36646b3bb3fa1cdd6; Actions 38555940573 passed required CI/deploy. Five-seat Duel source/target/counter priority was regression-tested.

**Gate B — complete, 2026-10-14:** viewer-private options and submitted intents, immutable common deadline, stale/duplicate/racing submits, disconnect implicit Pass and unaffected ordinary response time. Implementation/test changes a899cf4d792298c0c17bd65ceedb53de82d4caf4 plus CI repair 31e385c94140442df18509d8b35d6ef1bfb91216; focused privacy API 19/19 and Stauchness 12/12; Actions 38557911474 succeeded.

**Gate C — complete, 2026-10-14:** Local Dock shows each viewer's own pending/submitted choice without public intent card; server-confirmed winning card remains the only public Negation. Bounded polling, durable negationWindowId, validated privacy/room and 390/440/480/wide browser screenshots. Commit e7b51a4949ebb9c1215ca29db57ddab91691f460; focused build, 59/59 API, 16/16 authenticated Worker/D1 browser cases and Actions 38581603361 passed. **Aggregate real-device user visual acceptance remains pending**; CI is not acceptance.

**Historical source-only review (before latest user trace):** The agent previously found no source-verifiable server protocol defect, and Actions run 38607911627 passed. **SUPERSEDED by user's actual gameplay/log report:** unable to Confirm in first ~4s, missing seat histories for repeated Negations, and unclear/publicly late winner reveal. These three failures are now §9.5 active P0. Green CI alone does NOT close that regression.

**Preserve delivered Gates A–C privacy/card conservation/seat-priority winner, while updating only the timing end condition under §9.5 N0.** Previous forced full-8s wait is superseded: 8s is a MAXIMUM, with early settle only after ALL living players explicitly submit an intent or Pass. This is a bounded server protocol update, not a wholesale Negation rewrite and not immediate-public UX3. A committed winning Negation follows §8.7 Reveal Policy B; private intents never interrupt.


### 9.5 Negation controls, public reveal and actor-attached history — N0–N3 delivered, user review open

**Source-grounded user defects (2026-10-14 trace) and latest user-approved all-seats early completion rule:**
1. Legal Negation card is selected, but Confirm stays disabled while the original Duel/Stratagem is shown large for about four seconds. A player has effectively only ~4s of a fixed 8s server window to respond.
2. The true publicly committed Negation and counter-Negation CardFaces are not consistently parked at the real authors' Seats/Docks. Duel's pre-exchange Negation chain can show only a latest-card/status view, losing the public back-and-forth history.
3. The server accepts a private Negation intent before the winner becomes public. Under the revised rule, the wait until **all living players explicitly submit OR the maximum 8s deadline** is legitimate; any **additional post-commit** visual queue delay is a defect. Do not reveal a mere private intent before authoritative settlement.

**Historical trace before early-closure change:** Window opened ~8097.920s; private Negation request ~8102.482s; HTTP 200 ~8102.688s; winning public Negation appeared ~8105.997s, consistent with the OLD forced-8s server deadline. The new policy requires tests of BOTH paths: explicit response from all seats before 8s and one silent seat requiring the deadline. Trace independent timestamps for open/deadline, last explicit response persisted (if all), authoritative settlement, public event arrival, first large CardFace paint, and real-seat park. Never leak private responders in public traces.

**Code checkpoints to inspect — probable UX wiring gaps, not guaranteed complete root-cause diagnosis:**
- In app/page.tsx, responsePresentationReady bypasses the visual barrier for currentAction.negationWindowId, BUT presentationBusy feeds activeSkillSelectionBusy and buildConsoleDecisionDisplay.busy, which can disable the primary Confirm. Follow the whole legal CurrentAction/responseSelectionComplete/consoleDecision.primary chain.
- duelNegationPublicAction currently builds a root + latest counter ID but not a full action.responses array. DuelCardJourney seat-attached history iterates action.responses. Compare proof-backed singleTargetNegationResponses and other family-specific presenter owners to ensure all committed cards (not intents) are rendered once.
- Inspect activePublicNegationRead, publicCounterRead, event identity, seen-event flags, scope changes, old reveal queues and polling. Winner should be shown promptly when its PUBLIC event arrives and may interrupt the older card under §8.7 Reveal Policy B; do not wait for another 8-second deadline or four-second old-card hold.

#### 9.5.1 Normative UX2 behavior — secret Plan A with approved all-seats early finish

1. **From window T0:** Every living player gets their own private Negation/Pass selection, with legal Hand and Confirm/Pass interactive even while the original Stratagem/Duel big card is still showing. The **maximum** deadline stays T0+8000ms; no visual wait steals interaction time. A player with no real Negation provider gets **Pass only**, which they must tap explicitly to enable early closure.
2. **Upon successful private Confirm:** Immediately show ONLY to that viewer "Response submitted — waiting", lock further submissions for this window, and preserve their Hand until a winning public card is chosen. Other players cannot learn whether this player submitted/held Negation, and NO public physical CardFace is created yet.
3. **At the FIRST of: all living seats' explicit responses durably accepted OR the immutable T0+8s maximum deadline:** Server atomically closes the window and selects one true winning Negation by canonical seat priority. No-card seats are NOT filtered or auto-Passed. Only at deadline can missing seats be counted as timeout/implicit Pass. Consume only the real winner card; losers keep their cards. If no winner, settle original effect with no fake card. A winner opens a new maximum-8s counter window and old losing intents never carry over.
4. **After either early/deadline PUBLIC winning commit:** Reveal the real Negation at centre promptly with no further old-reveal queue or wait for the next shared window. It interrupts the old root only on genuinely committed PUBLIC proof (§8.7) and then gets its own default up-to-4s reveal and ~380ms author-seat park. New confidential counter controls remain usable immediately.
5. **Complete author-attached history:** Every genuinely committed root Stratagem + each successive winning Negation/counter is shown in true global order and at its actual author's Seat/Dock through the relevant interaction. Reuse accepted §8.1 topology: top Seat = chronological vertical stack below Hero; side Seat = horizontal inward fan; local Dock = horizontal above. No hidden earlier counters, latest-only history, ghost duplicates, incorrect actors or private losing cards. Include accessible ordered history and optional compact ordinals.
6. **Timing semantics:** **Up to 8s** secret selection, optionally settled earlier **only on complete explicit participation**, differs from up-to-4s real PUBLIC CardFace reveal and ~380ms move. Only a newly committed public card can interrupt visual display. Private individual intent/Pass, poll, deadline animation or selection never causes a public card before settlement.
